# Review log — 0104_fix_two-gaps-leave-lenny-s-session-scoped-and-delegation

## Standing context

Changelog (orchestrator split-binding pass, after spec round 8): rewrote this section around the settled decisions D1 to D12. Removed every Settled, Trap, and Deferred entry that served the forwarded-call session binding (SPEC-1 Edit (f) as a binding, Design rule 4, CODE-5, its checklist step, its TEST-1 entry, its summary index line, and the D1 reason sentence), because D11 splits the binding out. Closed the checklist DEFERREDs for S3, S4, S5, and S6 and the S1/S2 forward-reference DEFERRED (no S1 or S2 edit cites a label SPEC-2 creates). Closed the problem-statement "mints no token" OPEN ("mints no JWT" applied). Merged the satoken.go and authposture.go comment OPENs into Deferred. The Ledger is unedited apart from the appended `[orchestrator.split-binding]` entry.

### Settled decisions

- **D1. Pods hold no Lenny-issued JWT.** The §10.2 boundary table, §10.3, and the §13.3 credential flow are canonical, and the code follows them. Rejected: pods hold Lenny JWTs so the §10.2 claims list wins (needs a delivery channel, a `subject_token` the gateway lacks on the pod path, 24-hour renewal, redelivery after restart or handoff); threading the user's JWT or bearer `jti` through `LNK-GWCONTROL` (§13.3 bars client tokens from pods).
- **D2. Session creation mints no JWT.** No implemented endpoint consumes one; the playground bearer keeps `typ: session_capability`. Rejected: signing and recording the token as a revocation anchor (KMS dependency and 24-hour `exp` on long sessions); returning it in the `POST /v1/sessions` response; minting it lazily at first delegation.
- **D3. The 0102 step (1a) refusal survives**, re-anchored on session creation (SPEC-1 Edit (b)); one function serves every root-creating handler and client-facing `lenny/delegate_task`. Rejected: a route enumeration (drifts); triggers "presented client bearer" and "Lenny-issued bearer".
- **D4. `exchange_type: session_mint` stays**, redefined in §16.7 as the caller-check rejection row. Rejected: a new enum value or renaming `DELEGATION_PARENT_REVOKED` (churn with no behavior gain).
- **D5. Internal delegation mints no token.** Child identity is the session row and the §8.3 lease. Rejected: routing child minting through the Token Service by HTTP or a new gRPC RPC (needs parent tokens the gateway does not store); binding a session to the creating bearer's `jti` (15-minute rotation breaks delegation).
- **D6. Parent freshness keys on the parent row**: the child insert reads the parent `FOR SHARE` and refuses a terminal or absent parent with the existing `CreationRefusal`; `ErrParentRevoked` is deleted. Rejected: a lock-free `INSERT ... WHERE EXISTS` (READ COMMITTED race); relying on the FK's `FOR KEY SHARE` (does not conflict with a non-key UPDATE).
- **D7. The fence refuses only a terminal parent.** Rejected: refusing every non-running parent; mapping admission's `ErrParentNotRunning` to `DELEGATION_PARENT_REVOKED`.
- **D8. Revocation reaches a tree through session-tree mechanisms** (§11.4 full revoke, the creation fence, the §8.10 cascade). Rejected: a session-tree recursive-revocation endpoint; making `RevokeCascade`/`RevokeBySubject` take the §11.7 advisory lock; a cascading `POST /v1/admin/issued-tokens/{jti}/revoke`.
- **D9. `DELEGATION_AUDIT_CONTENTION`, the §8.2 retry paragraph, and `ErrAuditContention` are deleted.** Rejected: re-anchoring the code on the `delegation.spawned` audit write (no spec rule puts it under the lock).
- **D10. 0104 lands after 0102's spec steps and 0102 CODE-2 item 7** (`SourceRefusal`).
- **D11. The forwarded-call session binding is split out.** SPEC-1 Edit (f) deletes the false §10.2 paragraph with no replacement; REC-1 stages an `ABSENT` claim-register row (generator `scripts/seed-claim-register.py`, `deferral_id: R14`) and a Medium BUILD-GAPS finding routed to its own proposal in plan §10.2; SPEC-2 "Child identity" states that a pod-path child's parent is the session ID the adapter presents. Rejected: the one-sentence binding rule of rounds 3 and 8 (no `pod_assignment` invariant across suspend, resume, recycle, and terminal; no RPC coverage; no posture for the dev plaintext listener with no SPIFFE identity; no assignment timing; no refusal outcome); clearing `pod_assignment` on every terminal write; keying on `REG-SLOTCOUNT` or the coordination lease; a delegation-only rule in SPEC-2.
- **D12. Runtime self-submission of evals is dropped.** SPEC-5 Edits (q) and (r) delete the §10.7 "via its session credential" caller and the §23.1 item 12 runtime caller; no pod-side eval path exists in code; a later proposal may add it through a platform tool. D2 says "no implemented endpoint consumes one" for this reason.

### Single-home rules

- Caller check: SPEC-1 Edit (b). Child identity: SPEC-2. Parent fence: SPEC-3 Edit (a). `session_mint` row content: SPEC-5 Edit (g). Every other site cites in one clause.
- `DELEGATION_PARENT_REVOKED` precedence lives in §12.8, SPEC-3 Edit (b) ("none of those codes matches"), plus the §15.1 catalog row (SPEC-4 (a)). SPEC-2 "Parent freshness" states no code. `details.parentSessionId` lives only in the SPEC-4 (a) row.
- Cascade primary read: the SPEC-3 (a) fence sentence, following the *Drain* precedent; CODE-2 item 7 implements it.
- The 0102 ordering lives in the summary **Impacts on other proposals** row 0102.
- The binding gap lives in the REC-1 claim-register row and BUILD-GAPS finding; no spec sentence states a binding.

### Verified code facts

- The session `uploadToken` is HMAC-SHA256 and a credential-lease token is an opaque handle; "no token" claims are scoped to JWTs (SPEC-1 (a) "Every Lenny-issued JWT").
- Every `LNK-GWCONTROL` request carries the projected SA token as `authorization: Bearer` (RequireSATokenInterceptor); CODE-3 rule 1.2 (empty `p.JTI`) is the pod-path discriminator. Do not write "presents no bearer".
- `platformtools.go` dispatches "under the session id the adapter presents"; `connectortools.Bridge.CallConnectorTool` resolves the session by ID with no pod check. AgentPeerVerifier has no Pool/PodName and is built only when `clientCA != "" && trustDomain != ""`.
- For the routed binding proposal: `Session.PodAssignment` holds the Sandbox name, which equals the pod name; it survives terminal writes and is cleared on resume_pending, awaiting_client_action, resume rebind, and suspension release; scrub reports name released sessions; `bind_registration.go` sets it late on one path (UNVERIFIED whether a tool call can precede it).
- `cascadeToChildren` (sessionserver/usage.go) lists the whole tenant with `ListFilter{}` and groups by ParentSessionID; pgstore `List` reads the replica unless `Primary`; memstore ignores `Primary`; no index on `sessions.parent_session_id`.
- A plain UPDATE takes FOR NO KEY UPDATE, which conflicts with FOR SHARE, so every terminal writer serializes against the child insert's parent read. Lock order: tenant, user, parent, all shared. During a Redis outage the §10.1 FOR UPDATE coordination fallback makes every child insert wait.
- Only the delegation service writes `delegation_depth > 0`; `a2a_delegation` roots stay 0, so the parent rule never fires on `POST /v1/sessions`.
- The child row commits before pod allocation; tree-budget and ID reservations are returned on `CreationRefusal`; the service inserts outside any parent Update mutator.
- Fence code treats full revoke as `users.deleted_at`; §8.10 step 5 cascades on every terminal state; orphan cleanup keys on the root being terminal.
- Terminal predicate: `pkg/session/state.IsTerminal`. Lease bounds are enforced by the Redis Lua admission script, independent of any mint.
- `token.exchanged` keeps `delegation_mint` for external `a2a_delegation`. OpenAPI, schemas/, sdks/, and charts/ carry no `session_capability`, `session_mint`, `DELEGATION_AUDIT_CONTENTION`, `DELEGATION_PARENT_REVOKED`, or `revocationReason`.
- `/internal/runtimes` (runtimes.go) takes any principal and filters by tenant grant; `/mcp/runtimes` is client-facing behind authmw.
- No Go type implements CallerIdentity/ActorClaim; "Shared Adapter Types" is a `####` heading directly under `## 15`.

### Traps

- Do not narrow delegation "mints no token": SPEC-2, SPEC-5 (b), D5, and the architecture.md bullet are about delegation and stay true.
- SPEC-1 Edit (b)'s second locator quotes 0102's staged text verbatim; keep it.
- Act and ActorClaim move together (SPEC-4 (d)); the Act comment is one five-line block; quote it whole. Deriving Act from the child row and deleting Act/ActorClaim were rejected.
- Keep `DELEGATION_PARENT_REVOKED` out of the §12.8 four-code order and out of SPEC-2; "none of those codes matches" carries the ranking.
- §18.2 row 9, §18.23, and §18.40 item 7 state one prerequisite; change all three. §18.40 items are cited only by anchor.
- §27.3.1 names its claims directly; §10.2 "JWT claims" has no `caller_type`.
- 0100 moves the `usage.go` post-terminal steps; whichever lands second keeps `Primary: true` on the cascade list.
- Tier 7a has no replica; only the tier-1 recording-store assertion on `ListFilter.Primary` discriminates.
- `*spec-changes.md` globs also match `.non-spec-changes.md`; use the leading dot.
- The claim register is generator-produced; a hand edit to `tests/claim-map.json` fails the tier-0 generator gate, and a non-`WIRED` row must name a declared plan step.
- Pre-existing, do not file: crash between terminal commit and cascade; `budget_reserve` without `budget_return` on a fence refusal; §13.3 citing §8.3 for recursive revocation; §15.2.1 item 5 naming; `INTERNAL_ERROR` versus `token_exchange_failed` (different paths).
- Pod-granularity concerns on concurrent-session pods belong to the routed binding proposal, not 0104.

- **UNVERIFIED CallerIdentity for a pod-path child**: SessionMetadata.CallerIdentity/Act has no stated source for a child created over `LNK-GWCONTROL`.

## Ledger

### [spec.1.fix-G1.1]
FACT: Lenny issues tokens that are not JWTs. The session-creation `uploadToken` is HMAC-SHA256 (`<session_id>.<expiry>.<hmac_hex>`), and a credential-lease token is an opaque handle that costs no KMS Sign call. Scope every "no token"/"every token" claim to JWTs. — EVIDENCE: spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:58; spec/17_deployment-topology.md:1301; spec/10_gateway-internals.md:211 "Every Lenny-issued JWT is signed with ES256"
DECISION: SPEC-1 Edit (b) gives one outcome for both caller-check infrastructure failures: an `issued_tokens` read failure or a rejection-audit write failure returns `500 INTERNAL_ERROR` and creates no session — BECAUSE the §13.3 by-reference 500 is the Token Service's `500 token_exchange_failed`, which is absent from the §15.1 catalog (INTERNAL_ERROR is at spec/15_external-api-surface.md:1047), and CODE-3 item 2, TEST-1 "Caller check" and DOCS-1 already use INTERNAL_ERROR — ALTERNATIVES: appending only a read-failure sentence (one check, two 500 codes); applying §13.3 by reference (session creation is no /v1/oauth/token exchange).
DECISION: Design rule 1 lost its "Session creation mints no token." sentence instead of being reworded, because the rule already names SPEC-1 Edit (b) as its home.
WATCHOUT: Do not narrow the delegation "mints no token" sentences (SPEC-2 Child identity, the SPEC-5 §4.3/§13.3 endpoint sentence, summary D5 and the rejected-alternative line, non-spec architecture.md bullet). The uploadToken belongs to session creation, and those sentences are about delegation, so they stay true. Edit (d)'s "mints no `session_capability` token" names a token type and also stays true. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-2 "**Child identity.**", SPEC-5 "Internal delegation through `lenny/delegate_task` mints no token"
WATCHOUT: The Edit (b) instruction sentence quotes 0102's text ending "or with the `500` response that paragraph states when the rejection audit write fails." as its locator. It must stay verbatim even though the replacement block no longer says that. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-1 Edit (b) instruction paragraph
OPEN: problem-statement.md first paragraph says "`POST /v1/sessions` mints no token". Read literally, that is false because of the uploadToken; "mints no JWT" is what is meant. The design marked it separate-finding, so it is left unedited for a later pass.

DECISION: §18.2 Phase Map row 9 cell goes into SPEC-5 Edit (l), replacing its false closing sentence, rather than a new Edit (m) — BECAUSE it is the same Phase 5.5 → Phase 9 prerequisite as §18.40 item 7, and §18.40's intro says the chain is restated on each phase — ALTERNATIVES: separate Edit (m) (splits one fact across two labels).
DECISION: §5.1 and §27.3.1 edits append to SPEC-5 as Edits (m) and (n); the §15 MCP endpoint boundary edit is SPEC-4 Edit (e) — BECAUSE SPEC-5 is the multi-file restatement block and spec/15 is SPEC-4's file; the boundary bullet sits under §15.1 > Session Replay Semantics — ALTERNATIVES: a new SPEC-6 (needs a new checklist step and index line); relabelling SPEC-5 into file order (breaks every "SPEC-5 Edit (g)" citation).
DECISION: §27.3.1 drops the comparison and names the claims directly ("carrying `user_id`, `tenant_id`, `caller_type`, and `expiry` claims plus") — BECAUSE the reviewer's pointer to §10.2 "JWT claims" is wrong: that list has no `caller_type` — EVIDENCE: spec/10_gateway-internals.md:218-224.
FACT: GET /internal/runtimes/{name}/meta/{key} accepts any authmw principal and matches on the caller's tenant grant; /mcp/runtimes/{name} is a client-facing endpoint authenticated by the auth middleware, not an agent-pod proxy — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/runtimes.go:267-300; pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcpruntimes/mcpruntimes.go:1-25,138-142; cmd/lenny-gateway/httpsurface.go:340.

OPEN: separate findings for a later round — (1) spec/07_session-lifecycle.md §7.4 "Upload authorization": "the caller's normal session-scoped bearer credential" names the token class SPEC-1 (d) removes; (2) docs/api/internal.md:61 "Pods cannot forge or extend session JWTs" is falsified by SPEC-1 (f) and no DOCS-1 bullet covers it; (3) cmd/lenny-gateway/authposture.go comment says /internal/runtimes "requires a session JWT" though the handler takes any principal; (4) §15 boundary bullet calls /mcp/runtimes an "agent-pod tool proxy" while the code serves it to clients.

### [spec.1.review-performance.1]
FACT: Every session-row write goes through pgstore.Update, which takes `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` on that row only and touches no tenant or user row, so the SPEC-3 parent `FOR SHARE` read (lock order tenant, user, parent) creates no lock cycle with terminal writers — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:534; fence reads at pgstore/creation_fence.go:41-43
FACT: The delegation Service inserts the child through store.Create/CreateReserved outside any parent Update mutator, so the parent FOR SHARE cannot self-block on the delegating replica — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegation/service.go:1538-1548
FACT: Net write-rate effect of 0104 is a reduction: the pre-0104 spec charged one KMS Sign plus one issued_tokens INSERT and one audit INSERT under the per-tenant audit advisory lock per session creation and per delegation (spec/10_gateway-internals.md:216, spec/17_deployment-topology.md:1301-1303); 0104 leaves one issued_tokens PK read per bearer-carrying session creation, and the advisory lock only on the rejection path (non-spec CODE-3 IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE).
DEFERRED [docs/runbooks/jwt-key-rotation.md]: Step 3 (line 267) sizes the Sign quota as "the sum of the session creation rate, the delegation rate, the token renewal rate ... and infrequent operator mints"; after SPEC-5 Edit (j) the sum is the renewal rate of concurrent holders plus operator mints. The page is not in DOCS-1's list.

The plan's 0097 owner decision ("a gateway-to-pod renewal path is a recorded follow-up") is consistent with the renewal non-goal. — EVIDENCE: gateway-runtime-comms-remediation.md:3286-3298, 3597-3600 (0097 decisions in §10.3); summary.md D1, D2, D5, Non-goals

### [spec.1.review-reliability.1]
FACT: §12.3 "Read replicas" routes "task tree" reads to replicas, and §8.10 never says the cascade's child listing reads the primary; only the §12.8 drain and the §11.4 full-revoke listing pin the primary. A fence guarantee of the form "an insert that commits first is visible to <listing>" holds only when that listing reads the primary. — EVIDENCE: spec/12_storage-architecture.md:146; spec/12_storage-architecture.md:811 (*Drain* "lists the scope's sessions from the Postgres primary, because a replica can omit a row"); spec/11_policy-and-controls.md (11.4 step 1 "reading the Postgres primary")
FACT: §8.10 orphan cleanup only catches nodes whose ROOT session is terminated, so a child missed by a non-root parent's cascade is not reclaimed while the root lives. — EVIDENCE: spec/08_recursive-delegation.md:1100
FACT: in code the child row commits (commitChild) before the pod is allocated and the tree-budget reservation is released by Delegate's deferred release on any later error, so "no child pod is allocated" on a fence refusal holds. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegation/service.go:1203-1217
WATCHOUT: the parent_session_id FK takes FOR KEY SHARE, which does not conflict with a non-key UPDATE (FOR NO KEY UPDATE); the explicit shared lock in SPEC-3 is what makes the serialization hold. Do not "simplify" it to rely on the FK.

FACT: After 0102 SPEC-2 Edits (a) and (b), the §10.2 "Signing topology" text matches 0104 SPEC-1 Edit (b)'s locators. 0102 Edit (b) writes the "(1), (1a), (2), and (3) ... 500 response" sentences that 0104 replaces. EVIDENCE: proposals/0102_*/0102_*.spec-changes.md:77-93

DECISION: I did not file the removal of the fail-closed token.exchanged audit for internal delegation (DELEGATION_AUDIT_CONTENTION) as a control regression — BECAUSE the proposal removes it explicitly (D9 and the non-goals), and delegation.spawned still audits each child — ALTERNATIVES: filing it as a silently removed mandatory control was rejected because the removal is stated rather than silent.
DECISION: I did not file the single-bearer-revocation gap (D8) — BECAUSE it is a stated design decision: a §11.4 full revoke and the creation fence still cut a tree off, and an impersonation session cannot be created — ALTERNATIVES: none.

### [spec.3.review-mechanism.1]
FACT: The sentence right after the §12.8 *Creation fence* code order, "On `POST /v1/sessions` and MCP `create_session` these codes are session-creation rejections under §15.2.1 rule 5", is NOT in SPEC-3 Edit (b)'s quoted old text. Adding DELEGATION_PARENT_REVOKED to the order therefore puts it under "these codes". §15.2.1 rule 5's matrix says "any future session-creation rejection added to that catalog MUST be added to this list in the same change", and each listed code must be "exercised with a canonical triggering input on `POST /v1/sessions`". A root session has depth 0, so this code cannot be triggered there. — EVIDENCE: spec/12_storage-architecture.md:811; spec/15_external-api-surface.md:1447
USEFUL [spec.1.review-applicability.1]: its OPEN on §15.2.1 rule 5 was the lead. Filed this round as a predicate-drift finding.
FACT: Postgres row-lock semantics back the SPEC-3 fence. A plain UPDATE takes FOR NO KEY UPDATE, which conflicts with FOR SHARE, so "a terminal transition holds an exclusive lock on the session row" holds for any SQL terminal write, bulk updates included. After the lock wait, a READ COMMITTED FOR SHARE re-reads the row version that committed. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-3 Edit (a)
FACT: §8.10 step 5 applies cascadeOnFailure on every parent terminal state (completed/failed/cancelled/expired), so the fence's "visible to the walk" covers all terminal writers. — EVIDENCE: spec/08_recursive-delegation.md §8.10 "Parent pod failure with active children" step 5 and "Cascading behavior"
FACT: ActorClaim.SessionID stays satisfiable after 0104, although no Lenny token carries `session_id` anymore. Its comment keeps "Empty for actors that are not themselves Lenny sessions". — EVIDENCE: spec/15_external-api-surface.md:266-267

### [spec.3.review-performance.1]
FACT: The shipped cascade walk lists the whole tenant, not the parent's children: `cascadeToChildren` calls `s.store.List(ctx, sess.TenantID, sessionstore.ListFilter{})` and groups the rows by ParentSessionID in memory. It runs on every terminal transition whose policy resolves to cancel_all, and `DefaultCascadePolicy = CascadeCancelAll`, so leaf and root sessions with no children trigger it too. No migration indexes `sessions.parent_session_id`. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/usage.go:739, :389; pkg/api/v1/session/session.go:224; migrations/0001_initial_schema.up.sql:85
DEFERRED [0104 non-spec-changes.md CODE-2 item 7]: Item 7 says "`cascadeToChildren` lists the tenant's sessions with `ListFilter{Primary: true}`". That moves a full-tenant scan from the read pool to the primary on every cancel_all terminal. At the top tier (§16.5: 2,000/s creation, which is about the same as the termination rate, and 100,000 concurrent sessions) a single large tenant costs up to about 2e8 rows/s on the primary. SPEC-3 Edit (a) needs only "lists the parent's children from the primary". CODE-2 item 7 should list the children by `parent_session_id` with `delegation_depth > 0`, on the primary, backed by an index. It should not keep the whole-tenant List. Because the spec text is correct, this was not filed as a spec finding.
FACT: §13.3 step (1a) is defined as a step inside the advisory-locked issuance transaction, after step (1). The SPEC-1 Edit (b) caller check applies only (1a), which is one `issued_tokens` SELECT, so session creation no longer takes the per-tenant audit advisory lock. That is lighter than the 0102 design, which recorded the token under statements (1)-(3). Only rejections write an audit row. EVIDENCE: spec/13_security-model.md:657
FACT: CreateWithTranscript holds the fence's FOR SHARE locks for the whole transcript copy, but it serves derive and replay (delegation_depth 0), so the new parent FOR SHARE is never held across a copy. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/pgstore/creation_fence.go:127-141

No plan-wide rule beyond that applies to this proposal. EVIDENCE: gateway-runtime-comms-remediation.md:3286-3298, BUILD-GAPS.md:14493-14499, BUILD-GAPS.md:9429

The "Shared uploadToken key proposal" (F-7.1.31) is a separate plan step that "may join"; 0104 does not claim it, so it is out of scope. EVIDENCE: gateway-runtime-comms-remediation.md:3299-3300

### [spec.3.review-reliability.1]
- FACT: Any plain UPDATE of the parent session row (pgstore.Update FOR UPDATE, drain forced transition, full-revoke cancel) takes a row lock that conflicts with the child insert's FOR SHARE, so SPEC-3 Edit (a)'s "terminal transition holds an exclusive lock" holds for every terminal writer, not only pgstore.Update. sessions has no deleted_at, so "absent" means no row. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:534; migrations/0001_initial_schema.up.sql (CREATE TABLE sessions, parent_session_id ON DELETE SET NULL)
- FACT: The delegation service already returns the tree-budget reservation and ends the ID reservation on a CreationRefusal, so the new DELEGATION_PARENT_REVOKED refusal leaks neither Redis budget nor the export. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegation/service.go:840, :1549, :1574
- WATCHOUT: A gateway crash between the parent's terminal commit and the in-process cascade walk leaves pre-commit children uncascaded; §8.10 orphan cleanup keys on the ROOT session being terminal, so children of a terminal mid-tree parent wait for the root. This gap predates 0104 (it applies to every child, not only the fence race) and SPEC-3 only claims visibility to the walk, so I did not file it. — EVIDENCE: spec/08_recursive-delegation.md:1099
- DECISION: returned no findings under the reliability lens — BECAUSE the staged fence, reservation-scope split, and caller-check 500 outcome each have a correct crash/refusal path in spec and code — ALTERNATIVES: filing the cascade-crash gap (pre-existing, remedy in §8.10, outside this proposal's edits).

### [spec.3.review-security.1]
FACT: With SPEC-1 Edits (d) and (f) applied, the spec has no rule that binds the session id a pod presents on LNK-GWCONTROL (forwarded CH-MCP-PLATFORM tools/call) to a session assigned to that pod. §10.3 checks pod identity only at the handshake (SPIFFE URI against SandboxClaim/Sandbox). The §10.2 boundary table names pod-level credentials only. The code dispatches under the session id the adapter presents (pkg/gateway/gatewaycontrol/platformtools/platformtools.go:20-22 "the bridge dispatches under the session id the adapter presents"). The deleted sentence "Pods cannot forge or extend this token. The gateway validates the signature on every pod→gateway request." was the only spec statement of a per-session binding. — EVIDENCE: spec/10_gateway-internals.md:204-209, :225, :254, :348
DECISION: filed this as a security finding (a pod self-report sets the parent identity of a pod-path child), with the fix in SPEC-2 "Child identity" or SPEC-1 Edit (b) — BECAUSE the spec treats a pod as possibly compromised (§13.2.3 residual exposure, §4.9 SPIFFE lease binding), and the proxy-mode bar rules out a pod self-report as the authority — ALTERNATIVES: treating it as pre-existing code drift (rejected: the spec stated a control and 0104 deletes that statement without a replacement).
FACT: Checked for regressions and found none: the caller check fails closed on read and audit failure; the parent fence reads under FOR SHARE in the insert transaction on the primary; lease bounds (maxDepth, fan-out, budget) are enforced by the gateway at §8.2 step 2, not only at the Token Service mint. — EVIDENCE: spec/08_recursive-delegation.md:56, :81-89
UNVERIFIED: spec/13_security-model.md:693 "Delegation-lifetime preservation" still talks about "the parent session's delegation lease" for "delegation child minting". After 0104 only a2a_delegation goes through the Token Service. An edit-sites reviewer should decide whether this is stale.

### [spec.6.review-performance.1]
FACT: The SPEC-3 parent-row FOR SHARE read adds one shared row lock per delegated-child insert (Tier 3 delegation 500-1,000/s per spec/08_recursive-delegation.md:390). The existing fence already takes shared locks on the tenant row and the user row for every session insert, and the tenant row is hotter. So the parent lock adds no new hot key or serialization point. Parent and child rows are co-located under the existing StoreRouter condition. EVIDENCE: spec/12_storage-architecture.md:811 ("reads the tenant row and the row of the user ... under a shared row lock"; "A StoreRouter that places a tenant's session rows on an instance without its users' rows must replace this mechanism")
FACT: The SPEC-1 Edit (f) forwarded-call binding adds no net store dependency. The CallPlatformTool and CallConnectorTool handlers already resolve the session per call (ErrSessionNotFound), so the assignment check can share that read, and its Postgres-failover behavior matches the shipped handlers. LNK-GWCONTROL reaches any replica rather than the coordinator, so the check cannot depend on coordinator memory. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegationtree/leasecontrol/platformtools.go:108-112; spec/28_communication-channels.md:108
FACT: 0104 removes the per-session-creation issued_tokens write and the KMS Sign call, which lowers Postgres write and KMS load. EVIDENCE: spec/16_observability.md:607-620; spec/17_deployment-topology.md:1301-1307

### [spec.8.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: The session-creation caller check's trigger is stated by pointing at the §10.2 boundary table: a bearer presented at the "Client → Gateway" or "Automated clients" boundary. The LNK-GWCONTROL exemption sentence in SPEC-1 Edit (b) is deleted. BECAUSE the pod presents its projected SA token as a bearer on every pod-to-gateway request, so "presented bearer" covered the pod path and the exemption contradicted the trigger. The table classifies callers already, and §10.2 itself uses this wording ("per the Client→Gateway and Automated-clients rows of the boundary table above", apiKey playground bullet). ALTERNATIVES: "presented client bearer" was rejected because it is an undefined term and keeps the trigger-plus-exception structure. "Lenny-issued bearer" was rejected because CODE-3 checks OIDC bearers with a JTI too. An enumeration of routes was rejected because it drifts.
WATCHOUT: Do not reintroduce "a pod-path child presents no bearer" anywhere. It is false: the GatewayControl server runs leasecontrol.RequireSATokenInterceptor, and the pod sends `authorization: Bearer <jwt>`. Code discriminates the pod path by an empty Principal.JTI (CODE-3 rule 1.2), and that stays correct. EVIDENCE: spec/10_gateway-internals.md:209 (Pod → Gateway row); pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegationtree/leasecontrol/satoken.go:27; cmd/lenny-gateway/gwcontrol_server.go (RequireSATokenInterceptor)
FACT: The trigger now has one home, SPEC-1 Edit (b). Design item 1, edge cases "Pod-path delegation" and "Client-facing lenny/delegate_task", and summary D3 only point to it. Parent-row governance of pod-path children points to SPEC-2 "Child identity". EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md design item 1, the edge-case bullets, and summary.md D3

### [spec.8.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: SPEC-1 Edit (f) predicate now reads "only when the session the request names is not in a terminal state and its pod assignment (§4.2) is the pod that the caller's SPIFFE identity names (§10.3)"; "It refuses every other such request before dispatch." unchanged — BECAUSE no terminal transition clears Session.PodAssignment, so a terminal row on a reused or keep pod still names that pod and would pass a pod-only check, serving another session's identity and connector credentials — ALTERNATIVES: clear pod_assignment on every terminal write (lifecycle change touching release, scrub, stale-slot and orphan paths; out of scope); key on REG-SLOTCOUNT or the coordination lease (concurrent-pool only, Redis fallback, LNK-GWCONTROL reaches any replica); require `running` (refuses live non-running states that still hold the pod; D7 chose terminal-only for the fence); restore a pod-held token (contradicts D1).
FACT: PodAssignment is cleared only on resume_pending, awaiting_client_action, resume rebind, and suspension release; terminal rows keep it. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/failure.go:416,459; resume_rebind.go:209; suspend_release.go:173,471; spec/04_system-components.md:517 "whose pod no active session references"; spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:680 index `WHERE state = 'active'`.
WATCHOUT: Edit (f) is the one home of the liveness conjunct. Do not restate it in Design rule 4, the summary, or SPEC-2, and do not add a terminal-write lock clause to Edit (f); the §12.8 fence already covers delegate_task. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md Design item 4 (pointer only).

### [spec.8.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: SPEC-1 Edit (b) states the caller-check trigger by pointing at the §10.2 boundary table ("a bearer presented at the "Client → Gateway" or "Automated clients" boundary of the table above") and deletes the LNK-GWCONTROL exemption sentence outright — BECAUSE a trigger keyed to the boundary table excludes the pod's SA token by its own terms, so no exception clause is needed and none can contradict the trigger again — ALTERNATIVES: reviewer's "presented client bearer" + "presents no client bearer" (rejected: same answer one step narrower, keeps the exception clause, and "client bearer" is a term the spec never defines); keying on "Lenny-issued bearer" (rejected: CODE-3 reads issued_tokens for any principal with a JTI, including OIDC bearers, so the 500-on-read-failure scope would diverge from code).
DECISION: design index item 1 (spec-changes.md) drops its restatement of the trigger and keeps only name + Home, like item 4; summary D3 drops the clause after the colon and cites SPEC-1 Edit (b); edge case "Client-facing lenny/delegate_task" drops its "because it creates a session on a presented bearer" description — BECAUSE each was a second statement of the predicate, and two of them already drifted into the contradiction this round filed.
FACT: every pod→gateway request on LNK-GWCONTROL carries the projected SA token as `authorization: Bearer` metadata, so "presents no bearer" is false for the pod path — EVIDENCE: spec/10_gateway-internals.md §10.2 boundary table row "Pod → Gateway"; §10.3 "**Projected SA token:**" ("validates the audience claim on every pod→gateway request"); pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegationtree/leasecontrol/satoken.go.
WATCHOUT: CODE-3 rule 1.2 ("An empty p.JTI returns nil. This is the pod path over LNK-GWCONTROL") stays correct after this fix; do not rewrite it to mention client bearers — it is the code-side discriminator and agrees with the boundary-table trigger.

### [spec.8.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: close the Edit (f) liveness gap by adding a non-terminal conjunct to the one predicate in the Edit (f) code block ("only when the session the request names is not in a terminal state and its pod assignment (§4.2) is the pod that the caller's SPIFFE identity names (§10.3)") — BECAUSE terminal transitions never clear the binding, so a reused or `keep` pod still matches a terminal session's row; the conjunct reads a column the check already reads — ALTERNATIVES: clearing pod_assignment on every terminal write (touches every terminal writer, and the release, scrub, and orphan paths that read terminal rows; outside 0104's problem); keying on REG-SLOTCOUNT membership or the coordination lease (per-slot set exists only for maxConcurrentSessions > 1, Redis-outage fallback, and LNK-GWCONTROL reaches any replica, so coordinator memory is unavailable); `running`-only (refuses tool calls in live non-running states that still hold the pod; D7 chose terminal-only for the same reason on the fence).
FACT: no terminal transition clears Session.PodAssignment. It is cleared only on resume_pending, awaiting_client_action, resume rebind, and suspension release — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/failure.go:416,459; resume_rebind.go:209; suspend_release.go:173,471. The spec matches: suspension "clears the session's pod binding" (spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:230) and §4.6.1 filters orphan detection by "active session" (spec/04_system-components.md:517), so terminal rows are the only rows that still name a pod they no longer occupy.
FACT: the session terminal predicate is pkg/session/state.IsTerminal (Completed, Failed, Cancelled, Expired) — EVIDENCE: pkg/session/state/state.go:44-58
WATCHOUT: a forwarded call that passes the check just before the parent's terminal write is still served. No lock is needed: lenny/delegate_task is caught by the §12.8 parent-terminal fence rule (SPEC-3), and other tool calls in flight at the terminal transition are no different from any other in-session action. Do not add a serialization clause to Edit (f).
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md]: the CODE-5 interceptor already deferred by earlier entries (review log, the "Forwarded tool calls act only for a session on the calling pod" DEFERRED) must also refuse with PermissionDenied when the resolved session's state is terminal (pkg/session/state.IsTerminal), from the same GetByID read as PodAssignment, before dispatch. Its TEST-1 tier-1 entry gains one case: a terminal session whose PodAssignment equals the caller's SPIFFE pod name reaches no handler. That case is the discriminating assertion for the liveness conjunct.
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md]: the docs/api/internal.md:61 replacement already deferred to DOCS-1 (reader-facing statement of the forwarded-call binding) must say the gateway serves a forwarded tool call only for an unfinished session assigned to the calling pod.

### [spec.8.review-security.1]
DECISION: filed SPEC-1 Edit (f) as a security design defect: the forwarded-call binding checks only that the named session's pod_assignment equals the caller's SPIFFE pod, with no liveness conjunct — BECAUSE a terminal session row keeps naming its pod (the spec's live-binding queries filter on `state = 'active'`, spec/04_system-components.md:517, spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:680), and a reusing recycling pool serves later sessions on the same pod/runtime process (spec/05:495-500), so a later session's runtime can name an earlier terminal session and pass the check; and the pre-0104 durable cut-off (full revoke step 5 revoking the pod-held session token in issued_tokens) is gone — ALTERNATIVES: requiring pod_assignment to be cleared at every terminal write (larger, touches §7/§5.2 text owned elsewhere).
FACT: LNK-GWCONTROL carries only the forwarded CH-MCP-PLATFORM / CH-MCP-CONNECTOR calls; CH-ADAPTEREVENTS rides LNK-POD-GRPC (gateway-dialled), so Edit (f) covers every pod-originated request on the pod-dialled link — EVIDENCE: spec/28_communication-channels.md:107,124-128
FACT: agent SPIFFE URIs are per pod (`spiffe://<td>/agent/{pool}/{pod-name}`), so a per-pod binding is expressible — EVIDENCE: spec/10_gateway-internals.md:343,348
FACT: in code only the failure edges clear PodAssignment (resume_pending, awaiting_client_action); completion/cancel do not — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/failure.go:416,459
WATCHOUT: `ls proposals/0102*/*spec-changes.md` also matches the non-spec-changes file; name the spec-changes file exactly.

### [spec.8.review-single-source.1]
FACT: The forwarded-call session binding SPEC-1 Edit (f) writes has no prior statement in spec/. The only SPIFFE binding rules are the CH-LLMPROXY lease binding (§4.9, restated in §28.5.5, §28.6, §28.8), and the adapter-side single-session check on CH-MCP-PLATFORM/CH-MCP-CONNECTOR in §28.6 is a different rule. Edit (f) is the sole home. EVIDENCE: spec/28_communication-channels.md:1946-1961, spec/28_communication-channels.md:1575-1579
FACT: The parent-terminal fence (SPEC-3 Edit (a)) reuses the marker-serialization argument from the same §12.8 paragraph but applies it to the parent session row. It is a separate rule rather than a copy. SPEC-2 "Parent freshness" refers to it in one clause. EVIDENCE: spec/12_storage-architecture.md:811
DECISION: returned no single-source findings in round 8 — BECAUSE each staged rule (caller check, child identity, parent fence, forwarded-call binding, session_mint row content) has one stating site, and every other site is a cite or a one-clause summary — ALTERNATIVES: considered reporting "session creation mints no JWT", which appears in SPEC-1 Edit (b), Edit (d), and SPEC-5 Edit (g). Rejected because each is a one-clause fact, which is below the (g) bar.

- FACT: The cascade runs on every terminal whose policy resolves to cancel_all, and cancel_all is the default (`DefaultCascadePolicy = CascadeCancelAll`), so it fires on roots with no children too. — EVIDENCE: pkg/api/v1/session/session.go:224,243; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/usage.go:389,735-739
- DECISION: filed the Deferred "CODE-2 item 7 moves a full-tenant scan to the primary" item as a finding (top-tier math: §16.5 Tier 4 2,000/s sustained creation means about 2,000 terminal scans/s of the whole tenant, history included, landing on the single primary) — BECAUSE the directive says to apply Deferred items in this loop, and CODE-2 item 7 still stages `ListFilter{Primary: true}` — ALTERNATIVES: adding a parent_session_id index (needs a migration that the staging says it does not have; idx_sessions_root already covers the delegation tree).
- WATCHOUT: switching the cascade to a root-keyed listing stops cascading to derived sessions (their parent_session_id points at the source but their root is themselves). §8.10 covers the delegation tree only, so this is probably correct, but the implementor should confirm it and the TEST-1 "Cascade read source" entry must assert the new call. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/derive.go:416
- USEFUL [Standing context Deferred, CODE-2 item 7]: correct, and it saved the trace. CORRECTS it in one respect: no new index is needed, because idx_sessions_root already serves the tree listing.

FACT: commitChild runs undoChildExport (export delete, then ReleaseReservation, on a detached context) only for a CreationRefusal on the reserved path; a non-refusal error such as a failed parent read keeps the reservation, which is the existing behaviour for tenant and user read errors — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegation/service.go:1544-1578

### [non-spec-recheck.5.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: The parent-state-to-`ParentTerminal` mapping is a store concern (CODE-2 items 3 and 5, **Trap: the terminal set**), so its test cases live in the store-tier TEST-1 entries. **Postgres fence** holds the case list (terminal refuses, no row refuses, a live state other than `running` admits). **memstore parity** cites it. The serialization test gets only the terminal case. BECAUSE `CreationMarkers.Refusal` takes `ParentTerminal` as an input, so the tier-1 rule model cannot see a parent row, and the absent-row and D7 boundaries exist only in `readCreationMarkers` and memstore `fence.go` — ALTERNATIVES: adding absent and live cases to **Fence rule model** (rejected: the rule model sets the bool directly, so those cases test nothing); restating the mapping in the test entry (rejected: CODE-2 owns it); adding the absent and live cases to the serialization test (rejected: neither one is a marker write that can race an insert).
WATCHOUT: In the **Fence rule model** focused test, "a missing parent refuses" uses the wrong level of vocabulary, because tier 1 never sees a parent row. That wording is how the absent-parent gap stayed hidden. Replace it with "`ParentTerminal` on a delegated child". — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/creation_fence_test.go:17-99 (oracle over markers only)

FACT: The live non-terminal states are `suspended`, `resume_pending`, `awaiting_client_action`, and `resuming`. `state.Terminal()` is {completed, failed, cancelled, expired}. — EVIDENCE: pkg/session/state/state.go:25-48

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]

FACT: `cascadeToChildren` takes ONE tenant listing after the root parent's terminal commit, builds `byParent` from it, and queues every cancelled descendant's children from that same map; each descendant's post-terminal hook gets `archiveSettledChild` as its signals tail, so no descendant runs a cascade walk of its own. A grandchild a still-running child delegates after the listing and before that child's cancel write is admitted by the fence (child running) and never cancelled. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/usage.go:739-789 (`s.store.List(ctx, sess.TenantID, sessionstore.ListFilter{})`, `queue = append(queue, byParent[cur.ID]...)`), terminal_hook.go AfterTerminalWrite `signals` param.
FACT: §8.10 step 5 applies the cascade to every parent reaching a terminal state, including a descendant the cascade cancelled, so SPEC-3 Edit (a) "the parent's `cascadeOnFailure` walk ... lists the parent's children from the Postgres primary" already covers a cancelled child as parent. The spec text needs no change; only CODE-2 item 7 under-implements it. — EVIDENCE: spec/08_recursive-delegation.md:1069-1070, :1115.
DECISION: CODE-2 item 7 lists from the primary once per tree level, after that level's terminal writes return, and queues a descendant's children only from a listing taken after its terminal write committed — BECAUSE it closes the depth>1 hole with depth+1 listings (bounded by maxDepth) and stays inside one function — ALTERNATIVES: per-node relisting (reviewer's wording; O(nodes x tenant) primary scans with no parent-keyed read); handing each cancelled descendant `terminalSignals`/a recursive cascadeToChildren in its hook (changes archive/event/detach-cap behavior per descendant and collides with 0100's move of the post-terminal steps); a fixed-point re-walk after the BFS (same cost, harder to state).
DECISION: Fence race moves to a sibling parent-terminal race in creation_fence_race_test.go that reuses `memFenceStores`/`pgFenceStores` and runs no drain, rather than a `fenceMarker` or a branch inside `runFenceRound` — BECAUSE `fenceMarker.write` gets no parent ID and `runFenceRound` always drains and checks `checkFenceInvariant`, so a branch there is a conditional bolted onto a drain-shaped loop — ALTERNATIVES: lifecyclefence `World`/`PathDelegate` (runDelegate creates a fresh parent per create, delegate_path.go:30-35).
FACT: tier 7a pins the store serialization (admitted child visible to a primary listing taken as soon as the parent's terminal Update returns); the walk's per-level relisting is pinned only by the tier-1 Cascade read source grandchild case. — EVIDENCE: tests/tier7a_load_local/creation_fence_race_test.go:115-230.
WATCHOUT: Standing-context Deferred item "CODE-2 item 7 moves a full-tenant scan to the primary ... list children by `parent_session_id`" targets the same item-7 sentence. Whoever applies it keeps the per-level rule and swaps only the read (children of the level's IDs from the primary); do not revert to a single pre-walk listing. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-2 item 7.

FACT: CODE-3 caller check costs one PK read per root creation and client delegate_task (`issued_tokens.jti` is PRIMARY KEY; Get filters jti+tenant_id in one pgtenant.InTx). No Redis dependency, no write except on refusal. EVIDENCE: migrations/0001_initial_schema.up.sql:180-181; pkg/gateway/storage/issuedtokenstore/issuedtokenstore.go:380-384.
DECISION: returned no findings under the performance/failure-mode lens — BECAUSE the only new store load (parent FOR SHARE per child insert, PK read per creation) is O(1) indexed and fails closed the same way the shipped insert does under Postgres failover; Redis reset and coordinator handoff touch neither path; the cascade primary-read cost was already refuted on materiality — ALTERNATIVES: refiling the full-tenant primary scan (refuted, see history).

### [non-spec.3.fix-G1.1]

FACT: `lenny/cancel_child` runs its own §8.10 walk. `cancelSubtree` (mcptools.go) builds `byParent` once from the handler's `all`, which is a `ListByRoot` taken before any terminal write. pgstore `ListByRoot` reads the replica ("§12.3 task-tree projection routes to the read replica"). The cancelled nodes' signals are only `archiveCancelled`, so `cascadeToChildren` never runs for this path. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcptools/mcptools_register.go:599-629; mcptools.go:2281-2320; pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:738-747

One sentence keeps the handler's pre-write `ListByRoot` for `isDescendant` authorization and `treeRoot`. TEST-1 **Cascade read source** runs both cases against both walks (cancel_release_test.go for mcptools). BECAUSE no existing text covered cancelSubtree, and widening the one home avoids a second statement of the rule. ALTERNATIVES: reroute cancel_child through cascadeToChildren (changes the synchronous `cancelled` result, archive function, orphan-cap fallback, and collides with 0100 in usage.go); a primary variant of `ListByRoot` (new Store surface on pgstore, memstore, and fakes); a separate item 8 or Trap (states the rule twice).
WATCHOUT: Do not delete or move the cancel_child handler's `ListByRoot` to the primary when removing cancelSubtree's `all` queue source. It feeds `isDescendant` and `treeRoot`. EVIDENCE: mcptools_register.go:599-619
WATCHOUT: The Standing context Deferred entry about item 7's full-tenant primary scan now also applies to `cancelSubtree` (`ListFilter{Primary: true}` per level). Whoever applies that deferral covers both walks. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-2 item 7

### [non-spec.3.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: CODE-2 item 7 stays the one home of the per-level primary-listing rule and is re-subjected to both §8.10 cascade walks (`cascadeToChildren` in sessionserver/usage.go and `cancelSubtree` in mcptools/mcptools.go), both using `ListFilter{Primary: true}`; TEST-1 "Cascade read source" runs its two cases against both walks — BECAUSE SPEC-3 Edit (a) governs every cascadeOnFailure walk and cancel_child is a second implementation of it — ALTERNATIVES: (a) delete cancelSubtree's descendant walk and let the target's post-terminal hook run terminalSignals/cascadeToChildren: rejected, it changes the cancel_child `cancelled` result (descendants are cancelled async on the hook goroutine), swaps archiveCancelled for archiveSettledChild, adds the orphan-cap fallback to cancel_child, and collides with 0100's move of the usage.go post-terminal steps; (b) a primary variant of `ListByRoot`: rejected, new Store interface method on every implementer (pgstore, memstore, test fakes) for a scan cost the history refuted on materiality (non-spec.r3/r5 history "full-tenant session scan").
FACT: cancel_child takes one `ListByRoot` before any write (mcptools_register.go:600), pgstore.ListByRoot reads `s.read` (pgstore.go:746-747), cancelSubtree builds `byParent` once from it (mcptools.go:2285-2290, queue at :2312-2318), and the cancelled nodes' hook gets only archiveCancelled as signals (mcptools_register.go:622-627), so AfterTerminalWrite (terminal_hook.go:66-90) never reaches cascadeToChildren (usage.go:389). EVIDENCE: as cited.
WATCHOUT: the cancel_child handler's `ListByRoot` must stay: it feeds `isDescendant` authorization and `treeRoot` for the archive key. Only the walk's queue source moves to the primary listing. EVIDENCE: mcptools_register.go:600-627
WATCHOUT: Files touched lists each file once; the CODE-2 attribution for mcptools.go and mcptools_register.go lands on the mcptools line (non-spec-changes.md Files touched, first mcptools bullet), not on the CODE-2 sessionstore line.
UNVERIFIED: review-mechanism.3 notes `lenny/cancel_session` (mcptools cancelSession) and sessionserver expireSession/failSession run the post-terminal hook with no cascade at all; a pre-existing §8.10 step 5 gap outside 0104. Whoever routes BUILD-GAPS should confirm.

### [non-spec.3.review-performance.1]
FACT: Every session-row write in pgstore goes through `Update`, which takes `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` before a single `UPDATE sessions` (no other `UPDATE sessions` statement exists outside tests). FOR UPDATE already conflicts with the FK's FOR KEY SHARE, so CODE-2's parent `FOR SHARE` read adds no new conflict with any shipped session writer. Concurrent sibling inserts share the lock, so they do not serialize on a hot parent. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:491,534; migrations/0001_initial_schema.up.sql:85
FACT: The §10.1.1 Redis-outage fallback (`SELECT ... FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` on the session row) is not implemented for sessions. The only SKIP LOCKED sites are pod claims. If it were implemented, the FK lock would already block child inserts the same way, so 0104 cannot make this worse. EVIDENCE: spec/10_gateway-internals.md:29; grep "SKIP LOCKED" pkg (agentpodstate, podregistry, podsession only)
FACT: The CODE-3 caller check adds one primary PK read of `issued_tokens` per root creation that carries a JTI. At Tier 4 that is about 2,000/s (spec/16_observability.md:598). Each creation already runs a fenced Postgres insert, so the check adds no new failure dependency. The pod-path `delegate_task` does no read. `requireActiveUser` has only the four creation callers. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/{create,create_and_start,derive,replay}.go
DECISION: no performance or failure-mode findings this round. BECAUSE the parent lock, the caller-check read, and the KMS removal each either equal or improve on the shipped behavior. ALTERNATIVES: re-filing the per-level full-tenant primary cascade scan. I rejected that because the history marks it refuted on materiality, and "once per tree level" is a variant of it.

### [non-spec.5.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: returned no findings. BECAUSE the r4->r5 delta (CODE-2 item 7 extended to the cancel_child walk `cancelSubtree`, the Cascade read source test run against both walks, spec-map §8.2 pruning, the REC-1 tier-11 records test edit) adds no non-idempotent retry, unbounded wait, or abandoned resource beyond pre-existing behavior. ALTERNATIVES: filing that per-level primary re-listing adds a List-error point per tree level that leaves running descendants under a terminal parent. Rejected, because the same abandonment already exists for a mid-walk write error (cancelSubtree returns the IDs cancelled so far and the error; cascadeToChildren `continue`s), and it is the same class as the Standing context's "crash between terminal commit and cascade" pre-existing item.
FACT: cancelSubtree is the only walk for `lenny/cancel_child`. Its cancelNode hook passes `archiveCancelled` as the signal tail, and cascadeToChildren passes `archiveSettledChild`. Neither runs `terminalSignals`, which is the only caller of cascadeToChildren, so a node a walk cancels gets no cascade of its own, and the walk's BFS is the only thing that reaches its children. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcptools/mcptools.go:2360-2364; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/usage.go:332,389,778-781
WATCHOUT: a client retry of cancel_child after a partial walk returns TARGET_TERMINAL and does not resume the walk, because the handler checks `session.IsTerminal(child.State)` before cancelSubtree. FACT: the §8.2 spec-map entry lists all nine `_spec_13_3` tests, and the §13.3 entry already has every one of them, along with issuedtokenstore and tokenservice. Only childtoken is missing from §13.3, and CODE-1 deletes that package. EVIDENCE: tests/spec-map.json (sections "8.2" and "13.3")

The round-3 client-surface reviewer recorded this as a FACT and did not file it. Filed this round. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/route_table.go:45, create_handler.go:118-128
USEFUL [non-spec.3.review-security.1]: its root-creating-path inventory and its cross-tenant ErrNotFound WATCHOUT saved a full re-trace.

### [non-spec.7.review-performance.1]
FACT: The CODE-2 parent `FOR SHARE` read adds no new serialization against non-terminal parent updates. `pgstore.Update` already takes `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` on the session row, and that lock conflicts with the `FOR KEY SHARE` the `parent_session_id` FK takes at child INSERT. Parent updates and child inserts therefore already serialize in the shipped tree. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:534
FACT: The CODE-3 caller check reads `issued_tokens` through `issuedtokenstore.New(w.pgPool)`, which is the primary pool, so a just-written impersonation row cannot be missed to replica lag. The cost is one indexed primary read per root session creation and per client-facing delegate_task. The pod path skips the read under rule 1.2. The check adds no Redis dependency, and it fails closed on a Postgres outage, where session creation fails anyway. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/issuedtokenstore/issuedtokenstore.go:90,380
WATCHOUT: The cascade and cancel_child per-level tenant-wide primary scans have been refuted twice on materiality (history r7). Do not refile them under the performance lens.

The 0097 owner decision that "a gateway-to-pod renewal path is a recorded follow-up" is the only other plan text that touches it. — EVIDENCE: gateway-runtime-comms-remediation.md:3263-3265, :3286-3298, :3598-3600

### [non-spec.7.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: returned no findings — BECAUSE the r7 delta is two rewordings (CODE-3 Sequencing trap, TEST-1 Caller check cases reduced to "one per rule and branch", summary row 0102) that add no retry, lock, or recovery path, and the CODE-2 fence and item-7 walks traced cleanly — ALTERNATIVES: filing a lock cycle on the new parent FOR SHARE (rejected: only pgstore.Update locks sessions FOR UPDATE, its tx touches no tenant or user row, and DeleteByUser/DeleteByTenant lock sessions then session_reservations, which the child insert locks after the parent read, so no cycle).
FACT: Reserve shares fenceInsert and the delegation service calls it with delegatedChild=true (pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegation/service.go:1405), so CODE-2 item 3's trigger "When delegatedChild is true" is narrowed only by item 4's "Reserve passes an empty parent ID and reads no parent row"; an implementor must gate the parent read on a non-empty parent ID — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/pgstore/creation_fence.go Reserve → fenceInsert(ctx, tx, tenantID, ownerUserID, delegatedChild). Not filed: item 4 states the outcome explicitly and the memstore parity test pins the reservation admit.
USEFUL [review-log lines 725, 818, 1416]: closed the Redis-outage FOR UPDATE fallback stall hypothesis without rework.

### [non-spec.7.review-security.1]
FACT: Every root-creating entry point in the tree reaches requireActiveUser. REST create, create-and-start, derive, and replay call it directly. MCP lenny/create_session goes through CreateSessionService, which replays handleCreate on a synthetic request whose ctx carries the caller's authmw principal (and so its JTI). The single-shot translators always get sessionSingleShotBinder, which goes through CreateAndStartService. The noopSingleShotBinder that calls store.Create directly is a unit-test and in-memory fallback, and production wiring never selects it. The only other store Create/CreateReserved caller is the delegation service. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/service.go:153-166,228-240; cmd/lenny-gateway/credentialsurface.go:63-66; pkg/gateway/environment/translator/open_responses.go:227
FACT: Principal.JTI is copied from the verified JWT jti claim. Today the impersonation route uses impersonation.NewMemStore with no pool gate. CODE-3 rule 1.1 (a nil IssuedTokens skips the check) is safe only because 0104 lands after 0102, whose D17 registers no impersonation route without a pool. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/middleware/auth/auth.go:569; cmd/lenny-gateway/adminrouter.go:424-435; proposals/0102_*/…summary.md:32
DECISION: Filed no security findings in round 7. BECAUSE the parent fence reads the Postgres parent row in the insert transaction, which is a trusted and durable source with no Redis role. The caller check fails closed on a read error and on an audit-write error, and it covers every creation entry point. Dropping the revCache pre-check removes no control: on the pod path JTI is empty, and on the client path the auth middleware checks revocation per request. ALTERNATIVES: filing nil-IssuedTokens fail-open (rejected because 0102 D17 bounds it) or the interceptor running before the delegate_task caller check (rejected because it is not a mandatory control and the check still runs before any store write).
